\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\int\int\int\frac{d(x_{1},\partial M)^{-4\delta}+d(x_{2},\partial M)^{-4\delta}+d(y_{1},\partial M)^{-4\delta}+d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-4\delta}}{|x_{1}-y_{1}|^{3-\delta}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{3-\delta}|x_{1}-y_{2}|^{2-2\delta}}

(6.62)

\displaystyle=:J_{4}(a)+J_{4}(b)+J_{4}(c)+J_{4}(d).

(6.63)

We will analyze
J_{4}(a)
, the other terms follow from similar considerations. Integrating out
y_{1}
, then
x_{2}
and then
y_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle J_{4}(a)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}\int\int\int\frac{d(x_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{3-2\delta}|x_{1}-y_{2}|^{2-2\delta}}

(6.64)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime\prime}\int\int\frac{d(x_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{1}-y_{2}|^{2-2\delta}}\leq C_{4}\int d(x_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}\leq C_{5}.

(6.65)

∎

Lemma 6.5
.

For every
\alpha\in(0,1)
, there exists
C>0
such that, for every admissible boundary law
\mathbb{P}^{0}
and every
T>0
,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[|\Upsilon_{2,T}^{b}(W^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+},Z^{0}_{-})|^{\alpha}]\leq C+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}+\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}].

(6.66)

Proof.

By symmetry we have that
\Upsilon^{b}_{2,T}=N_{1}+N_{2}
, where

\displaystyle N_{1}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3}H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(y)dxdy

(6.67)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2\int\gamma_{T}^{M}H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(x)dx,

(6.68)

\displaystyle N_{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{12^{2}}{3}\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(y)dxdy-\int\gamma_{T}^{M}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(x)^{2}dx.

(6.69)

We will only estimate
N_{1}
as the estimate for
N_{2}
follows by similar arguments. Recall the interpolation estimate of Proposition
B.3
,

|C^{M}_{T}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\delta}}{|x-y|^{1+2\delta}}.

(6.70)

Using this, the definition of
\gamma_{T}^{M}
, and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle|N_{1}|

\displaystyle=\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\left|\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x,y)^{3}H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})(x)\left(H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(y)-H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(x)\right)\right|

(6.71)

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int d(x,\partial M)^{\delta}|H(W_{-}^{0},W_{+}^{0})(x)|\frac{|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(y)-H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(x)|}{|x-y|^{3+2\delta}}

(6.72)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\left(\int\int\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{2\delta}|H(W_{-}^{0},W_{+}^{0})(x)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{3-2\delta}}dxdy\right)^{1/2}

(6.73)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left(\int\int\frac{|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(y)-H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(x)|}{|x-y|^{3+6\delta}}\right)^{1/2}

(6.74)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}\|d(x,\partial M)^{\delta}H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})\|_{L^{2}}\|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})\|_{H^{6\delta}}

(6.75)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime\prime}(\|W_{-}^{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}+\|W^{0}_{+}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2})+\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{6\delta}_{z}}^{2}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{6\delta}_{z}}^{2}.

(6.76)

The lemma now follows by combining the estimate above together with Proposition
4.6
to bound the moments of norms of
W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+}
that appear.
∎

6.3
Stochastic estimates on
\Upsilon_{3,T}

Recall that

\Upsilon_{3,T}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}):=-\frac{12^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\int^{T}_{0}\int(J_{t}(W_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket))^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x+\delta^{4,M}_{T}.

(6.77)

We will prove a
fractional moment estimate
on
\Upsilon_{3,T}
when
\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}
are i.i.d. distributed according to an admissible law on boundary conditions.

Lemma 6.6
.

For every
\alpha>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that, for every admissible boundary law
\mathbb{P}^{0}
and every
T>0

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[|\Upsilon_{3,T}(W^{0}_{-}+Z^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+}+Z^{0}_{+})|^{\alpha}]\leq C+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}].

Furthermore, the pushforward under the regularized boundary measure
(\Upsilon_{3,T})^{*}(\nu^{0}_{T}\otimes\nu^{0}_{T})
converges in law to a random variable

(\Upsilon_{3,\infty})^{*}(\nu^{0}\otimes\nu^{0})
.

Lemma
6.6
follows from Lemmas
6.7
and
6.8
below. As before, we split
\Upsilon_{3,T}=:\Upsilon_{3,T}^{a}+\Upsilon_{3,T}^{b}
, where
\Upsilon_{3,T}^{a}
depends only on
W^{0}_{-}
and
W^{0}_{+}
. First note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int^{T}_{0}\int J_{t}(W_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}t{\mathrm{d}}x=\frac{1}{2}\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x,y)^{2}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket^{2}(x)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket^{2}(y).

(6.78)

Thus, we define

\Upsilon^{a}_{3,T}:=-\frac{12^{2}}{4}\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x,y)^{2}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket^{2}(x)\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket(y)+\delta^{4,M}_{T}.

(6.79)

Lemma 6.7
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{0}\otimes\mu^{0}}[|\Upsilon^{a}_{3,T}|^{2}]\leq C.

(6.80)

Proof.

As before, we may take expectations with respect to
\tilde{\mu}^{0}
and use the infinitely smoothing correction to the covariance to extend the estimates to
\mu^{0}
. Expanding and using the definition of
\delta^{4,M}_{T}
together with Wick’s theorem, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}^{0}\otimes\tilde{\mu}^{0}}[|\Upsilon_{4,T}^{a}|^{2}]

(6.81)

\displaystyle=\frac{3\cdot 12^{3}}{2}\int\int\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})^{2}C^{M}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})^{2}E_{\tilde{\mu}^{0}\otimes\tilde{\mu}^{0}}\Big[\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket^{2}(x_{1})\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket^{2}(y_{1})

(6.82)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket^{2}(x_{2})\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket^{2}(y_{2})\Big]-(\delta_{T}^{4,M})^{2}

(6.83)

\displaystyle=\frac{3\cdot 12^{3}}{2}\int\int\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})^{2}C^{M}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})^{2}\Bigg\{(2!)^{2}C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},x_{2})^{2}C^{B}_{T}(y_{1},y_{2})^{2}

(6.84)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\cdot 2^{2}C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})C^{B}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},x_{2})C^{B}_{T}(y_{1},y_{2})\Bigg\}=:{\mathrm{I}}+{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}.

(6.85)

By the covariance estimates on
C^{M}_{T}
and
C^{B}_{T}
, see Appendices
B
and
C
, together with standard bounds on convolutions, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\int\int\frac{1}{|x_{1}-y_{1}|^{2}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{2}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{2}}

(6.86)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\int\int\frac{1}{|x_{2}-y_{1}||x_{2}-y_{2}|^{2}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{2}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\int\int\frac{1}{|x_{2}-y_{1}|^{2}}\leq C^{\prime\prime\prime}.

(6.87)

For the second term, we will additionally use the boundary covariance interpolation estimate of Proposition
B.3
. For every
\delta>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\int\int\frac{d(y_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{1}-y_{1}|^{3-\delta}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{3-\delta}|x_{1}-x_{2}||y_{1}-y_{2}|}

(6.88)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\int\int\frac{d(y_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{2}-y_{1}|^{1-\delta}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{3-\delta}|y_{1}-y_{2}|}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\int\int\frac{d(y_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{2-2\delta}}\leq C^{\prime\prime\prime}.

(6.89)

∎

We now turn to estimating
\Upsilon^{b}_{3,T}
.

Lemma 6.8
.

For every
\alpha>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that, for every admissible boundary law
\mathbb{P}^{0}
and every
T>0

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[|\Upsilon_{3,T}^{b}(W^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+},Z^{0}_{+})|^{\alpha}]\leq C+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}].

Furthermore, the pushforward under the regularized boundary measure
(\Upsilon_{3,T}^{b})^{*}(\nu^{0}_{T}\otimes\nu^{0}_{T})
converges in law to a random variable

(\Upsilon_{3,\infty}^{b})^{*}(\nu^{0}\otimes\nu^{0})
.

Proof.

We will only prove the estimate since the convergence statement holds by similar arguments as with
\Upsilon^{b}_{2,T}
. We may write

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{b}_{3,T}

\displaystyle=-\frac{12^{2}}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq 2\\
0\leq j\leq 2\end{subarray}}\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{2}{2\choose i}{2\choose j}

(6.90)

\displaystyle\times\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-i}_{T}\rrbracket(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}_{T}(x)\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-j}_{T}\rrbracket(y)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{j}_{T}(y).

(6.91)

We will treat
j=0
and
j\geq 1
separately and call these
N_{0}
and
N_{\geq 1}
respectively. Additionally, we will split
N_{\geq 1}=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 2,j\geq 1}N_{\geq 1}(i,j)
. Furthermore, let us drop the subscript
T
from the harmonic extension terms for ease of notation.

First, let us consider
j\geq 1
and fix
i=1,2
. Then by the covariance interpolation inequality of Proposition
B.3
and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle|N_{\geq 1}(i,j)|

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\frac{1}{|x-y|^{3-2\delta}}d(x,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}|\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-i}\rrbracket(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}(x)|

(6.92)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times d(y,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}|\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-j}\rrbracket(y)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{j}(y)|dxdy

(6.93)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-i}\rrbracket H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}\|_{L^{2}}

(6.94)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-j}\rrbracket H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{j}\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.95)

Now since
i,j\geq 1
, we are considering at most the first Wick power and can treat the terms symmetrically. Thus, by splitting into
\tau
and
z
variables, and applying a uniform bound on the integrals,

\displaystyle\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-i}\rrbracket H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}\|_{L^{2}}

(6.96)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}\|H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-i}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\Big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}.

(6.97)

We now use the interpolation estimates (
C.4
). For every
\tau
,

\displaystyle d(\tau,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}\|H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-i}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\leq Cd(\tau,\partial M)^{-2\delta}(\|W^{0}_{-}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}+\|W^{0}_{+}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}).

(6.98)

Hence,

\displaystyle\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-\delta}

\displaystyle\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2-i}\rrbracket H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}\|_{L^{2}}

(6.99)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(\|W^{0}_{-}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}+\|W^{0}_{+}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}})\left\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{-2\delta}\|H(Z_{-}^{0},Z_{+}^{0})^{i}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}.

(6.100)

Observe that

\left\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{-2\delta}\|H(Z_{-}^{0},Z_{+}^{0})^{i}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}=\left\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{-2\delta}\|H(Z_{-}^{0},Z_{+}^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2i}_{z}}^{i}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}.

Thus, by Sobolev embedding and the fact that
2i\leq 4
, we have that

\Big\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{-2\delta}\|H(Z_{-}^{0},Z_{+}^{0})^{i}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\Big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\leq C\left\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{-2\delta}\|H(Z_{-}^{0},Z_{+}^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{1/2}_{z}}^{i}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}.

(6.101)

Thus, by trading regularity for boundary blow-up (see (
C.4
), we have that

(
6.101
)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\left\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{-(2+i)\delta}\|H(Z_{-}^{0},Z_{+}^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}^{i}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}

(6.102)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}(\|Z_{-}^{0}\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}^{i}+\|Z_{+}^{0}\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}^{i}).

(6.103)

It remains to treat the case
j=0
. By the kernel estimates in Appendix
B
and Young’s convolution inequality, we have that

\displaystyle|N_{0}(i)|

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\frac{1}{|x-y|^{3-\delta}}|d(x,\partial M)^{1/2-2\delta}H(W_{-}^{0},W_{+}^{0})^{2-i}(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}(x)|

(6.104)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times|d(y,\partial M)^{1/2+\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}\rrbracket(y)|

(6.105)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-2\delta}H(W_{-}^{0},W_{+}^{0})^{2-i}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}\|_{L^{2}}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2+\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.106)

The first product on the righthand side we have done above, so we will only estimate the second term. Note that by splitting into
(\tau,z)
integration, multiplying and dividing by
d(\tau,\partial M)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2+\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}\rrbracket\|_{L^{2}}

(6.107)

\displaystyle\leq\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1/2+\delta}\|\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}\rrbracket(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}

(6.108)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\||d(\tau,\partial M)|^{1+\delta/2}\|\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}\rrbracket(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}.

(6.109)

The lemma now follows by combining the estimates above and using Proposition
4.6
to bound the moments of norms of
W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+}
that appear.
∎

6.4
Stochastic estimates on
\Upsilon_{4,T}

Recall that

\Upsilon_{4,T}:=-\frac{4^{2}}{2}\int^{T}_{0}\int(J_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}_{T}\rrbracket)^{2}+\delta^{5,M}_{T}.

(6.110)

We will prove a fractional moment estimate on
\Upsilon_{4,T}
when
\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}
are i.i.d. distributed according to an admissible law on boundary conditions.

Lemma 6.9
.

For every
\alpha>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that, for every admissible boundary law
\mathbb{P}^{0}
and every
T>0

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[|\Upsilon_{4,T}(W^{0}_{-}+Z^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+}+Z^{0}_{+})|^{\alpha}]\leq C+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}].

(6.111)

Lemma
6.9
follows from Lemmas
6.10
and
6.11
below. As before, we split
\Upsilon_{4,T}=:\Upsilon_{4,T}^{a}+\Upsilon_{4,T}^{b}
, where
\Upsilon_{4,T}^{a}
depends only on
W^{0}_{-}
and
W^{0}_{+}
. As a preliminary, note that by Fubini’s theorem and the self-adjointedness of
J_{t}
, we have that

\int_{0}^{T}\int(J_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{3}\rrbracket)^{2}dt=\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{3}\rrbracket(x)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{3}\rrbracket(y).

(6.112)

Hence let us define

\Upsilon^{a}_{4,T}:=-\frac{4^{2}}{2}\int\int C_{T}(x,y)\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})_{T}^{3}\rrbracket(x)\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{3}_{T}\rrbracket(y)+\delta^{5,M}_{T}.

(6.113)

Lemma 6.10
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{0}\otimes\mu^{0}}[|\Upsilon^{a}_{4,T}|^{2}]\leq C.

(6.114)

Proof.

Again, it is sufficient to prove (
6.114
) with the expectation taken with respect to
\tilde{\mu}^{0}\otimes\tilde{\mu}^{0}
. By Wick’s theorem and the definition of
\delta^{5,M}_{T}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}^{0}\otimes\tilde{\mu}^{0}}[\left(\Upsilon_{4,T}^{a}\right)]

\displaystyle=4^{3}\int\int\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})C^{M}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})

(6.115)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mu}^{0}\otimes\tilde{\mu}^{0}}\Bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{2}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})_{T}^{3}\rrbracket(x_{i})\prod_{i=1}^{2}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})_{T}^{3}\rrbracket(y_{i})\Bigg]-(\delta^{5,M}_{T})^{2}

(6.116)

\displaystyle=4^{3}\int\int\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})C^{M}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})\Bigg\{2\cdot(3!)^{2}C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},x_{2})^{3}C^{B}_{T}(y_{1},y_{2})^{3}

(6.117)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 2^{2}C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})C^{B}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},x_{2})^{2}C^{B}_{T}(y_{1},y_{2})^{2}

(6.118)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+2\cdot(3!)^{2}C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})^{2}C^{B}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})^{2}C^{B}_{T}(x_{1},x_{2})C^{B}_{T}(y_{1},y_{2})\Bigg\}

(6.119)

\displaystyle=:{\mathrm{I}}+{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}+{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}.

(6.120)

Note that above, subtracting
(\delta^{5,M}_{T})^{2}
corresponds we have subtracting the most divergent term

4^{3}\cdot(3!)^{2}\int\int\int\int C^{M}_{T}(x_{1},y_{1})C^{M}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})C^{B}_{T}(y_{1},x_{1})^{3}C^{B}_{T}(x_{2},y_{2})^{3}.

We begin with the estimate of
{\mathrm{I}}
. Let
\delta>0
be sufficiently small. Using the kernel estimates on the Poisson kernel
C^{B}_{T}
and Dirichlet covariance
C^{M}_{T}
contained in Appendices
B
and
C
, together with standard bounds on convolutions, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\int\int\frac{d(x_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{1}-y_{1}||x_{2}-y_{2}||x_{1}-x_{2}|^{3-\delta}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{3-\delta}}

(6.121)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\int\int\frac{d(x_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|y_{1}-x_{2}|^{1-\delta}|x_{2}-y_{2}||y_{1}-y_{2}|^{3-\delta}}\leq C_{2}\int\int\frac{d(x_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{2-2\delta}}\leq C_{3}.

(6.122)

Now consider
{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}
. Again, by the covariance estimates ,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\int\int\frac{1}{|x_{1}-y_{1}|^{2}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{2}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{2}}

(6.123)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\int\int\frac{1}{|y_{1}-x_{2}|^{1}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{2}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{2}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\int\int\frac{1}{|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{2}}\leq C_{3}.

(6.124)

We now turn to
{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}
. By the covariance estimates, for
\delta>0
sufficiently small we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\int\int\frac{d(y_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{1}-y_{1}|^{3-\delta}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{3-\delta}|x_{1}-x_{2}||y_{1}-y_{2}|}

(6.125)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\int\int\frac{d(y_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|x_{2}-y_{1}|^{1-\delta}|x_{2}-y_{2}|^{3-\delta}|y_{1}-y_{2}|}\leq C_{2}\int\int\frac{d(y_{1},\partial M)^{-\delta}d(y_{2},\partial M)^{-\delta}}{|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{2-2\delta}}\leq C_{3}.

(6.126)

∎

We now turn to estimating
\Upsilon^{b}_{4,T}
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
\mathbb{P}^{0}
be an admissible law on boundary conditions
(W^{0},Z^{0})
. For every
\alpha>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that, for every
T>0

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[|\Upsilon_{4,T}^{b}(W^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+},Z^{0}_{+})|^{\alpha}]\leq C+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{0}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{0}}[\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{2}].

(6.127)

Proof.

Let us write

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{b}_{4,T}=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq 3\\
0\leq j\leq 3\end{subarray}}{3\choose i}{3\choose j}\frac{4^{2}}{2}N(i,j),

(6.128)

where

\displaystyle N(i,j)

\displaystyle:=\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})_{T}^{3-i}\rrbracket(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})_{T}^{i}(x)

(6.129)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})_{T}^{3-j}\rrbracket(y)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})_{T}^{j}(y)dxdy.

(6.130)

We will drop the subscript
T
in the harmonic extension terms below for ease of notation.

We begin by estimating when
j\geq 1
. By the covariance estimates of Appendix
B
and Young’s convolution inequality,

\displaystyle|N(i,j)|

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{1-\delta}d(y,\partial M)^{1-\delta}}{|x-y|^{3-2\delta}}|\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{3-i}\rrbracket(x)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}(x)|

(6.131)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad|\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{3-j}\rrbracket(y)H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{j}(y)|dxdy

(6.132)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1-\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{3-i}\rrbracket H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}\|_{L^{2}}

(6.133)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1-\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{3-j}\rrbracket H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{j}\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.134)

Due to the symmetric roles of
i
and
j
when
j\geq 1
, the following estimates are sufficient to conclude. Let us first assume
i=1
. Then

\displaystyle\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1-\delta}

\displaystyle\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}\rrbracket H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})\|_{L^{2}}

(6.135)

\displaystyle\leq C\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1+\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{2}\rrbracket\|_{L^{\infty}}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-2\delta}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.136)

The first factor on the righthand side can be treated by using Proposition
4.3
and properties of
H
. For the second factor, arguing as in preceding estimates, we have that

\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-2\delta}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}).

(6.137)

Let us now assume
i=2
. In this case, we estimate

\displaystyle\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1-\delta}

\displaystyle H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{2}\|_{L^{2}}

(6.138)

\displaystyle\leq\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2+\delta}H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-2\delta}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{2}\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.139)

As before, the first term on the righthand side can be estimated by properties of
H
. For the second term, notice that by splitting into
(\tau,z)
coordinates, using the 2d Sobolev embedding and using the positive exponent on the distance to boundary, we have that

\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-2\delta}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{2}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-2\delta}\|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{1/2}}^{2}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}},

(6.140)

and this can be estimated as above.

Finally, we assume
i=3
. By splitting into
(\tau,z)
integration and using Sobolev embedding, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1-\delta}H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{3}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&=\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1-\delta}\|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{6}_{z}}^{3}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\leq C\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1-\delta}\|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{2/3}_{z}}^{3}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}.\end{split}

(6.141)

Hence by exchanging regularity for boundary blow-up as above, we have that

\eqref{eq: upsilon4 b eq1}\leq C^{\prime\prime}(\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}^{3}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}^{3}).

(6.142)

Finally, we estimate when
j=0
, allowing for arbitrary
i\geq 1
. Then we have, by interpolating with the boundary blow-up as above,

\displaystyle|N(i,0)|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\||d(\cdot,\partial M)|^{1+\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{3-i}\rrbracket\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\times\||d(\cdot,\partial M)|^{3/2+\delta}\llbracket H(W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+})^{3}\rrbracket\|_{L^{\infty}}

(6.143)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\int\int\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{-1+\delta}d(y,\partial M)^{-1+\delta}}{|x-y|^{3/2+4\delta}}|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}(x)|

(6.144)

It remains to estimate the integral since the terms involving
W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+}
are sufficient for our purposes. Let us write
x=(\tau_{x},z_{x})
and
y=(\tau_{y},z_{y})
. By splitting into
(\tau,z)
integration and using that
|x-y|\geq|z_{x}-z_{y}|
, then integrating
z_{y}
and
\tau_{y}
first we have that

\displaystyle\int\int

\displaystyle\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{-1+\delta}d(y,\partial M)^{-1+\delta}}{|x-y|^{3/2+4\delta}}|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}(x)|dxdy

(6.145)

\displaystyle\leq C\int d(\tau_{x},\partial M)^{-1+\delta}\int|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})^{i}(\tau_{x},z_{x})|

(6.146)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\int d(\tau_{y},\partial M)^{-1+\delta}\int\frac{1}{|z_{x}-z_{y}|^{3/2+4\delta}}dz_{y}d\tau_{y}dz_{x}d\tau_{x}

(6.147)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int d(\tau,\partial M)^{-1+\delta}\|H(Z^{0}_{-},Z^{0}_{+})(\tau_{x},\cdot)\|_{L^{i}_{z}}^{i}d\tau\leq C^{\prime\prime}(\|Z^{0}_{-}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{3}+\|Z^{0}_{+}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa_{z}}}^{3}),

(6.148)

where we have used that
i\leq 3
so that by (the 2d) Sobolev embedding,
H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}\hookrightarrow L^{i}_{z}
.

The lemma now follows by combining the estimates above together with Proposition
4.6
to estimate the moments of the norms of the
W^{0}_{-},W^{0}_{+}
that appear.
∎

6.5
Proof of Theorem
3.20

We begin with convergence in law of the random variables
\Upsilon_{i,T}
. We will only consider the cases where the boundary fields are i.i.d. according to
\nu^{0}_{T}
or
\nu^{0}
, but one can generalize it to sequences of admissible boundary conditions that converge appropriately.

Lemma 6.12
.

For every
i=2,3,4
, we have that
\Upsilon_{i,T}(\varphi_{-}^{T},\varphi_{+}^{T})
, where
\varphi_{-}^{T},\varphi_{+}^{T}
are i.i.d. according to
\nu^{0}_{T}
, converges in law to a random variable
\Upsilon_{i}^{\infty}
.

The same convergence statement to the same random variables hold if
\varphi^{T}_{-}=(\varphi_{-})_{T}
and
(\varphi_{+})_{T}
, where
\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}
are i.i.d. according to
\nu^{0}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, let us consider
i=2
, the other statements follow by similar arguments. We will also only prove the first statement in the lemma, as the other follows similarly. We will work in the coupling measures where
\nu^{0}_{T}
is represented as the sum of
(W^{0}_{T},Z^{0}_{T})
and
\nu^{0}
is represented as the sum of
(W^{0}_{\infty},Z^{0}_{\infty})
. By Skorokhod embedding, we may take a subsequence (not relabelled) and assume that these random variables are defined on the same probability space and converge almost surely.

First of all, recall the splitting

\Upsilon_{2,T}(W^{0}_{-,T}+Z^{0}_{-,T},)=\Upsilon_{2,T}^{a}(W^{0}_{-,T},W^{0}_{+,T})+\Upsilon_{2,T}^{b}(W^{0}_{-,T},Z^{0}_{-,T},W^{0}_{+,T},W^{0}_{-,T}).

(6.149)

For the first term, the convergence statement follows from (
6.14
) and martingale convergence upon splitting
\Upsilon^{a}_{2,T}
into terms in homogeneous Wiener chaoses.

We now turn our attention to the second term. To do this, we view

\Upsilon_{2,T}^{b}
as a
fixed
multilinear functional of the
W^{0}
’s,
Z^{0}
’s,
and
the covariance
C_{T}^{M}
. Note that this can be done for every
\Upsilon_{i,T}
– the
\delta
renormalizations are absorbed in the
\Upsilon_{i,T}^{a}
term, and the mass renormalization term in
\Upsilon_{3,T}^{b}
can be rewritten in terms of
C^{M}_{T}
. By using the analytic arguments used to establish (
6.66
), see (
6.76
), we therefore have that these multilinear maps are bounded on the relevant Banach spaces. Thus, it follows from pointwise convergence of the
W^{0}
’s and
Z^{0}
’s, thanks to the Skorokhod embedding. The limiting random variable obtained is unique due to convergence to
W^{0}_{\infty},Z^{0}_{\infty}
, and thus we obtain the full convergence in law as required.

∎

We now prove Theorem
3.20
.

Proof of Theorem
3.20
.

This follows from Proposition
4.3
and Lemma
6.12
.

∎

7
Estimates on remainder terms

In this section, we will prove estimates on the remainder terms
R_{i}
. Recall that the bulk enhancement at scale
T
is the vector of stochastic processes
\Xi_{T}(W)
defined in Section
6.1
.

Proposition 7.1
.

For every
\delta>0
and for every
(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})
admissible boundary conditions, there exists
p=p(\delta)>0
and
C=C(\delta)>0
such that, for every
i=1,\dots,8
and every
T>0
,

\mathbb{E}[|R_{i}|]\leq C(1+\|\Xi^{\partial}_{T}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}}^{p})+\delta\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{C}_{T}(u)],\qquad\forall u\in\mathcal{H}.

(7.1)

Proposition
7.1
will follow from Lemmas
7.3
-
7.15
below. We will only prove the required estimates on the terms
R_{1},R_{3},R_{4},R_{8},R_{9}
. The terms
R_{5}-R_{7}
contain purely bulk terms and can be estimated as in
[
BG20
]
with straightforward modifications. The term
R_{2}
can also be estimated as in
[
BG20
]
with straightforward modifications.

Remark 7.2
.

Let us mention that the constant term on the righthand side of (
7.1
) comes from contributions that can be bounded by
\mathbb{E}[\|\Xi_{T}(W)\|_{\mathfrak{S}}^{p}]
. This yields a constant uniform in
T
in light of Proposition
6.1
. Strictly speaking our estimates are not fully deterministic, unlike for the periodic case
[
BG20
]
. We use the expectation to simplify estimates on terms involving the boundary field.

As preparation for these lemmas, we first establish regularity estimates on the boundary fields and on the drift term
K_{T}
induced by the intermediate ansatz.

7.1
Regularity estimates on harmonic extensions of boundary fields

The main result of this subsection, Lemma
7.3
below, is a collection of fine regularity estimates on the boundary fields. This is a quantitative extension of Proposition
4.3
to account for the
Z^{0}
term in the coupling representation of the boundary field. We will make heavy use of the regularizing properties of the harmonic extension described in Appendix
C
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
i\in{1,2,3}
. For every

\alpha\geq 0
such that
\alpha\in({\rm max}(0,\frac{i-2}{2}),i/2]
, and
\kappa>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C,p,\kappa^{\prime}>0
where
\kappa^{\prime}
is sufficiently small such that the following estimate holds. For every admissible boundary condition
(W^{0},Z^{0})
and for every
T\in(0,\infty]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[-L,L]}|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0}+Z^{0})_{T}^{i}\rrbracket\|_{\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-i/2+\alpha-\kappa}}

(7.2)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{\tau\in[-L,L]}|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})_{T}^{j}\rrbracket\|^{p}_{\mathcal{C}^{-j/2+\alpha-\kappa^{\prime}}_{z}}+\|(Z^{0})_{T}\|^{p}_{H^{1/2-\kappa^{\prime}}_{z}}.

(7.3)

Proof.

We will drop
T
from the notation when clear from context. The case
i=1
follows from Proposition
4.3
and the regularity properties of
Z^{0}
and harmonic extension.

We now treat the case
i=2
, in which case
\alpha\in(0,1]
. We split according to

\llbracket H(W^{0})^{2}\rrbracket+2H(W^{0})H(Z^{0})+H(Z^{0})^{2}.

(7.4)

The desired estimate on the first term follows from the stochastic regularity estimates of Proposition
4.3
.

For the mixed term, we will do a paraproduct decomposition in the
z
-direction, denoted
\succ_{z}
and also
\circ_{z}
for the resonant product. Provided
\alpha>\kappa
, we have

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\succ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\alpha-\kappa}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2+\alpha-\kappa/2}_{z}}\Big)\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa/2}_{z}},

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\Big(|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2+\alpha-\kappa/2}_{z}}\Big)^{2}+\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{1/2-\kappa/4}_{z}},

where in the last step we have used the 2d Besov embedding
H^{s+\kappa^{\prime}}_{z}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{s-1}_{z}
for
\kappa^{\prime}
arbitrarily small. The desired estimate now follows by properties of the harmonic extension.
For the resonant product term, we may then use the Besov embedding to obtain

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\circ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\alpha-\kappa}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\circ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{\alpha-2\kappa}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2+\alpha-\kappa}}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa}_{z}}.

The desired estimate then follows. The remaining paraproduct term can be estimated by similar arguments.

We now turn to the term quadratic in
Z^{0}
(still in the case
i=2
). For every
\kappa^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small, by Besov embedding there exists
C>0
such that

|d(x,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\alpha-\kappa}_{z}}\leq C|d(x,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{B^{1/2+\alpha}_{4/3-\kappa^{\prime},1,z}}.

(7.5)

Furthermore, by the fractional Leibniz rule and Sobolev embedding we have that for every
\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
sufficiently small and
\kappa^{\prime\prime}>0
sufficiently small depending on
\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}
, there exists
C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(x,\partial M)|^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{B^{1/2+\alpha}_{4/3-\kappa^{\prime},1,z}}

(7.6)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|Z^{0}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{z}}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{4-\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{z}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\|Z^{0}\|^{2}_{H^{1/2-\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{z}}.

(7.7)

The desired estimate follows.

Finally, let us do the estimates when
i=3
, in which case
\alpha\in(1/2,3/2]
. We expand:

\llbracket H(W^{0})^{3}\rrbracket+3\llbracket H(W^{0})^{2}\rrbracket H(Z^{0})+3H(W^{0})H(Z^{0})^{2}+H(Z^{0})^{3}.

(7.8)

The desired estimate on the first term is a consequence of Proposition
4.3
. For the second term, we will again do a (
z
-direction) paraproduct decomposition. First observe that, by Besov embedding, for every
\kappa^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\succ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-3/2+\alpha-\kappa}_{z}}

(7.9)

\displaystyle\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\succ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{-1+\alpha-\kappa^{\prime}}_{4-\kappa^{\prime},4-\kappa^{\prime},z}}.

(7.10)

Furthermore, by paraproduct estimates for every
\kappa^{\prime\prime}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(\varphi,W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\succ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{-1+\alpha-\kappa^{\prime}}_{4-\kappa^{\prime},4-\kappa^{\prime},z}}

(7.11)

\displaystyle\leq|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\alpha-\kappa^{\prime}}_{z}}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{4-\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{z}}.

(7.12)

The desired estimate follows since, by Sobolev embedding, for every
\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that
\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{4-\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{z}}\leq C\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{1/2-\delta}_{z}}
.

Let us now turn to the resonant product term. By Besov embedding, for every
\kappa^{\prime}>\kappa
sufficiently close to
\kappa
, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\circ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-3/2+\alpha-\kappa}}

(7.13)

\displaystyle\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\circ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H_{z}^{-1/2+\alpha-\kappa^{\prime}}}.

(7.14)

Furthermore by resonant product estimates, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\circ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{-1/2+\alpha-\kappa^{\prime}}_{z}}

(7.15)

\displaystyle\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|\llbracket H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\rrbracket\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\alpha-\kappa^{\prime}/2}_{z}}\|H(Z^{0})\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa^{\prime}/2}_{z}}.

(7.16)

The desired estimate now follows by arguments similar to above. The remaining paraproduct term follows by similar estimates as above.

We now turn to the third term and do another paraproduct decomposition. By paraproduct estimates, note that given
\kappa^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small, for every
\kappa^{\prime\prime}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\succ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{B^{-1/2-\kappa^{\prime}+\alpha}_{2-\kappa^{\prime},z}}

(7.17)

\displaystyle\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa^{\prime}/2+\alpha}_{z}}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{L_{z}^{2-\kappa^{\prime\prime}}}.

(7.18)

The lefthand side can be estimated (as above) using Besov embedding. The desired estimate holds by straightforward arguments. For the resonant product term, by Besov embedding and resonant product estimates, we have that for every
\kappa^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small there exists
C,C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\circ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-3/2-\kappa+\alpha}_{z}}

(7.19)

\displaystyle\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\circ_{z}H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{B^{-\kappa/2+\alpha}_{4/3-\kappa^{\prime},z}}

(7.20)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(W^{0})(\tau.\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa/4+\alpha}_{z}}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{2}\|_{B^{1/2-\kappa/4}_{4/3-\kappa^{\prime},z}}.

(7.21)

From here we may proceed as above. Finally, let us estimate the last term. First note that by Besov embedding, for every
\kappa^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that

|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{3}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-3/2+\alpha-\kappa}_{z}}\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{3}\|_{B^{\alpha}_{4/3-\kappa^{\prime},z}}.

(7.22)

By the fractional Leibniz estimate, interpolation estimate (
C.4
), and Sobolev embedding, for every
\kappa^{\prime\prime}>0
and
\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C,C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)^{3}\|_{B^{\alpha}_{4/3-\kappa^{\prime},z}}

\displaystyle\leq C|d(\tau,\partial M)|^{\alpha}\|H(Z^{0})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{\alpha}_{4-\kappa^{\prime\prime}}}\|Z^{0}\|_{L^{4}_{z}}^{2}

(7.23)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|Z^{0}\|_{H^{1/2-\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{z}}^{3}.

(7.24)

The desired estimate follows.

∎

7.2
Estimate on
K_{T}

Recall in (
5.43
) we introduced an intermedate ansatz

Z_{T}(u)=-\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}+K_{T}(u),

(7.25)

where

\displaystyle K_{T}(u)

\displaystyle=\int^{T}_{0}J^{2}_{t}(\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}\succ Z^{\flat}_{t})+J^{2}_{t}(\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})+12J^{2}_{t}W_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket

(7.26)

\displaystyle\qquad+4J^{2}_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{3}\rrbracket{\mathrm{d}}t+Z_{T}(\ell_{T}(u)).

(7.27)

The intermediate ansatz results in a loss of
H^{1}
regularity for
Z_{T}(u)
as
T\rightarrow\infty
since the non-drift term
\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}
has almost sure regularity
\mathcal{C}^{1/2-\kappa}
. It turns out that the remainder term
K_{T}(u)

almost
has the optimal regularity, as we shall now demonstrate.

Lemma 7.4
.

There exists
C>0
and
\kappa^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small such that, for every
\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}\in\mathfrak{X}
and for every
T>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|K_{T}\|_{H^{1-\kappa}}^{2}]

(7.28)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\Big(1+\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}\|_{L^{2-\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}+\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket\|_{L^{2-\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}

(7.29)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\|d(\cdot,\partial M)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}\rrbracket\|_{L^{2-\kappa^{\prime}}}^{2}\Big)+\mathbb{E}\left[\|Z_{T}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\ell_{T}(u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt\right].

(7.30)

Proof.

For the first term, we may estimate as in
[
BG20
, Section 7]
to obtain

\left\|\int^{T}_{0}J^{2}_{t}(\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}\succ Z^{\flat}_{t})dt\right\|_{H^{1-\kappa}}\leq\|\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\mathcal{C}^{-1-\kappa/2}}\|Z_{T}\|_{L^{2}},

(7.31)

which can then be bounded by Young’s inequality and stochastic estimates. Here, we have implicitly used the regularizing properties of
J_{t}
established in Appendix
B.4
.

For the second term, first observe that by the properties of
J_{t}
(again, see Appendix
B.4
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\left\|\int_{0}^{T}J^{2}_{t}(\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})dt\right\|_{H^{1-\kappa}}^{2}\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|J_{t}(\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})\|^{2}_{H^{-\kappa/2}}dt.

(7.32)

Hence, by Fubini’s theorem, regularizing properties of
J_{t}
, the integral kernel representation of Sobolev norms
13
13

13

Any function with Dirichlet boundary conditions is periodic and hence can be viewed as an element of the torus. Integral representations of (inhomogeneous) Sobolev norms using the kernel of the periodic Laplacian is standard. We have implicitly used the decay of the kernel in this estimate
, and kernel bounds on
C^{M}_{T}
(see Appendix
B
), we have that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\|J_{t}(\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})\|_{H^{-\kappa/2}}^{2}\right]

(7.33)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int\frac{|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(x)H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(y)|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}(x)\mathbb{W}_{t}^{2}(y)]}{|x-y|^{1-\kappa/2}}\frac{dt}{\langle t\rangle^{1+\kappa/4}}

(7.34)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int\frac{|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(x)H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(y)|}{|x-y|^{3-\kappa/2}}\frac{dt}{\langle t\rangle^{1+\kappa/4}}\leq C\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}\|_{L^{2-\varepsilon(\kappa)}}^{2},

(7.35)

where the last inequality is by Young’s convolution inequality and
\varepsilon
is a positive function of
\kappa
that is
o(1)
as
\kappa\downarrow 0
.

For the third term, by properties of
J_{t}
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left\|\int_{0}^{T}J^{2}_{t}(W_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket)dt\right\|_{H^{1-\kappa}}^{2}\Bigg]\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\kappa/2}\mathbb{E}[\|W_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket\|_{H^{-1-\kappa/2}}^{2}]dt.

(7.36)

It suffices to bound the expectation in the integrand uniformly in
t
. Note that, by Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|W_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket\|_{H^{-1-\kappa/2}}^{2}]

(7.37)

\displaystyle\quad=\int\int(-\Delta^{\rm per}+m^{2})^{-1-\kappa/2}(x,x^{\prime})C_{t}^{M}(x,x^{\prime})\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket(x)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket(x^{\prime})dxdx^{\prime}.

(7.38)

Thus by standard covariance estimates on the periodic Laplacian and the covariance estimates on
C^{M}_{T}
in Appendix
B
, for every
\alpha\in(0,1)
, there exists
C>0
such that

|(-\Delta^{\rm per}+m^{2})^{-1-\kappa}(x,x^{\prime})C_{t}^{M}(x,x^{\prime})|\leq C\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}d(x^{\prime},\partial M)^{\alpha}}{|x-x^{\prime}|^{1+2\alpha}|x-x^{\prime}|^{1-\kappa}}.

(7.39)

Hence,

\mathbb{E}[\|W_{t}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket\|_{H^{-1-\kappa/2}}^{2}]\\
\leq C\int\int\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}d(x^{\prime},\partial M)^{\alpha}}{|x-x^{\prime}|^{2+2\alpha-\kappa}}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket(x)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket(x^{\prime})dxdx^{\prime}.

(7.40)

Taking
\alpha=1/2
, then there exists
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\kappa)=o(1)
as
\kappa\downarrow 0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[\|J_{t}(W_{t}\llbracket H^{2}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}))\|_{H^{-\kappa/2}}^{2}]dt\leq C\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2}\llbracket H^{2}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\rrbracket\|_{L^{2-\varepsilon}}^{2},

(7.41)

as required.

For the final term, let us recall that
C^{M}_{T}=\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{2}\ast C^{M}_{\infty}
is a positive operator with square root
\sqrt{\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{2}}\ast(C^{M}_{\infty})^{1/2}
. We will use the regularizing properties of
(C^{M}_{\infty})^{1/2}
stated in Appendix
B.5
. By computing the
t
-integral and using regularizing properties of
(C_{\infty}^{M})^{1/2}
, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{0}^{T}J_{t}^{2}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}_{T}\rrbracket dt\Bigg\|_{H^{1-\kappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|C_{T}^{M}\left[\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}_{T}\rrbracket(y)\right]\right\|_{H^{1-\kappa}}^{2}

(7.42)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|(C_{T}^{M})^{1/2}\left[\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}_{T}\rrbracket\right]\right\|_{H^{-\kappa}}^{2}.

(7.43)

Let us define the kernel

\mathcal{C}(x,x^{\prime}):=(C_{\infty}^{M})^{1/2}\ast(-\Delta^{\rm per}+m^{2})^{-\kappa}\ast(C_{\infty}^{M})^{1/2}(x,x^{\prime}).

(7.44)

By the kernel estimatees on
(C_{\infty}^{M})^{1/2}
together with standard kernel estimates on
(-\Delta^{\rm per}+m^{2})^{-\kappa}
, we have htat

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}(x,x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}d(x^{\prime},\partial M)^{\beta}dydy^{\prime}}{|x-y|^{2+\alpha}|y-y^{\prime}|^{3-\kappa}|y^{\prime}-x^{\prime}|^{2+\beta}}

(7.45)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}d(x^{\prime},\partial M)^{\beta}dy}{|x-y|^{2+\alpha}|y-x^{\prime}|^{2+\beta-\kappa}}

(7.46)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime\prime}\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}d(x^{\prime},\partial M)^{\beta}}{|x-x^{\prime}|^{1+\alpha+\beta-\kappa}}.

(7.47)

We choose
\alpha=\beta=1-\varepsilon
for
\varepsilon
small enough depending on
\kappa
. Hence, by using the integral representation of Sobolev norms the definition of
\mathcal{C}(x,x^{\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|(C_{T}^{M})^{1/2}\left[\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}_{T}\rrbracket\right]\right\|_{H^{-\kappa}}^{2}

(7.48)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C^{\prime}\int\int|\mathcal{C}(x,x^{\prime})|\left|\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}\rrbracket(x)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}\rrbracket(x^{\prime})\right|dxdx^{\prime}

(7.49)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C^{\prime\prime}\int\int\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{1-\varepsilon}d(x^{\prime},\partial M)^{1-\varepsilon}}{|x-x^{\prime}|^{3-\kappa}}\left|\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}\rrbracket(x)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}\rrbracket(x^{\prime})\right|dxdx^{\prime}

(7.50)

\displaystyle\qquad=C^{\prime\prime\prime}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1-\varepsilon}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}\rrbracket\|_{L^{2-\varepsilon^{\prime}(\kappa)}}^{2},

(7.51)

where the last line follows by Young’s convolution inequality with
\varepsilon^{\prime}(\kappa)=o(1)
as
\kappa\downarrow 0
.

∎

7.3
Estimate of
R_{1}

Recall that

R_{1}=\int_{M}12W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}Z^{2}_{T}+6\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket Z^{2}_{T}\,dx=:R_{1}(a)+R_{1}(b).

(7.52)

We will begin with a useful interpolation estimate between Sobolev regularity and boundary blow-up.

Lemma 7.5
.

For every
\delta>0
, for every
\alpha,\beta>0
such that
\alpha+\beta<1
, there exists a constant
C
such that, for every
f\in H^{\alpha+\beta}(M)
,

\|d(x,\partial M)^{-\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}H^{\beta}_{z}}\leq C\|f\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta+\delta}_{x}}

(7.53)

Proof.

Note that
\langle n_{1}\rangle^{\alpha}\langle(n_{2},n_{3})\rangle^{\beta}\leq\langle(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\rangle^{\alpha+\beta}
. Hence

\|f\|_{H^{\alpha}_{\tau}H^{\beta}_{z}}\leq\|f\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta}}.

(7.54)

Therefore, by Hölder’s inequality and Sobolev embedding,

\displaystyle\|d(x,\partial M)^{-\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}H^{\beta}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int d(\tau,\partial M)^{-\frac{\alpha}{\alpha+\delta}}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2\alpha+2\delta}}\|\|f\|^{2}_{H^{\beta}_{z}}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{1-2\alpha-2\delta}}_{\tau}}

(7.55)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\|f\|^{2}_{H^{\beta}_{z}}\|_{H^{\alpha+\delta}_{\tau}}\leq C^{\prime}\|f\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta+\delta}}.

(7.56)

∎

We will estimate
R_{1}(b)
first since it is the more straightforward term to analyze.

Lemma 7.6
.

There exists
\kappa>0
sufficiently small such that, for every
\delta>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|R_{1}(b)|]

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\|d^{1-\kappa}(\cdot,\partial M)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket\|_{L^{4}_{\tau}L^{\infty}_{z}}^{4}

(7.57)

\displaystyle\qquad+\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-\kappa}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket\|_{L^{4/3}_{\tau}L^{\infty}_{z}}^{4}\Big)+\delta(\|K_{T}\|^{2}_{H^{1-\kappa}}+\|K_{T}\|^{4}_{L^{4}}).

(7.58)

Proof.

Recalling the intermediate ansatz (
5.43
), we further decompose
R_{1}(b)
into

R_{1}(b)=\int\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket(K_{T}^{2}+2\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}K_{T}+(\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]})^{2})=:\sum_{i=1}^{3}R_{1}(b;i).

(7.59)

Let us first estimate
R_{1}(b,1)
.
Let
\kappa>0
be sufficiently small. We split into
(\tau,z)
coordinates. By Hölder’s inequality, symmetry, followed by Hölder’s inequality again, we have

\displaystyle|R_{1}(b,1)|

\displaystyle\leq\int\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1-\kappa}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{-1+\kappa}K_{T}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|K_{T}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{4}_{z}}d\tau

(7.60)

\displaystyle\leq\|d^{1-\kappa}(\cdot,\partial M)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket\|_{L^{4}_{\tau}L^{\infty}_{z}}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-1+\kappa}K_{T}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|K_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{x}}

(7.61)

\displaystyle\leq C\|d^{1-\kappa}(\cdot,\partial M)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket\|_{L^{4}_{\tau}L^{\infty}_{z}}^{4}+\delta\left(\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-1+\kappa}K_{T}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|K_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{x}}^{4}\right).

(7.62)

The desired estimate holds by using Lemma
7.5
on the penultimate term of the righthand side.

For the second term, we proceed similarly. By Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle|R_{1}(b,2)|\leq\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2-\kappa}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket\|_{L^{4/3}_{\tau}L^{\infty}_{z}}\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-1/2+\kappa}\mathbb{W}^{[3]}_{T}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|K_{T}\|_{L^{4}_{x}}.

(7.63)

Similarly, for the final term we have

|R_{1}(b,3)|\leq\|d^{1-2\kappa}(\cdot,\partial M)\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{2}\rrbracket\|_{L^{1}_{\tau}L^{\infty}_{z}}\|d^{-1/2+\kappa}(\cdot,\partial M)\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.

(7.64)

The desired estimates follow by straightforward arguments.
∎

We now turn to the estimate of
R_{1}(a)
.

Lemma 7.7
.

There exists
p>0
such that, for every
\delta>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C=C(\delta)>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|R_{1}(a)|]

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}\|_{L^{8/5}}^{2}+\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1/2+2\kappa}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{p}

(7.65)

\displaystyle\qquad+\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1+2\kappa}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{1/2+\kappa}}^{p}\Big)+\delta(\|K_{T}\|^{2}_{H^{1-\delta}}+\|K_{T}\|^{4}_{L^{4}}).

(7.66)

Proof.

By Wick’s theorem,
\mathbb{E}[W_{T}(\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]})^{2}]=0
. Hence by the intermediate ansatz (
5.43
), we obtain

\displaystyle R_{1}(a)\equiv

\displaystyle\int W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}K_{T}{\mathrm{d}}x+\int W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})K^{2}_{T}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(7.67)

For the first term, by duality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have that for every
\delta>0
, there exists
C=C(\delta)>0
such that

\left|\int W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}K_{T}dx\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\left[C\|W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}\|^{2}_{H^{-1+\kappa}}+\delta\|K_{T}\|_{H^{1-\kappa}}^{2}\right].

(7.68)

Thus, in order to complete the lemma, we will analyze the expectation of the first term on the righthand side.

Recall that
\Delta^{\rm per}
is the Laplacian on
\mathcal{M}
. By the integral representation of Sobolev norms, we may write

\mathbb{E}\left[\|W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}\|^{2}_{H^{-1+\kappa}}\right]\\
=\int\int(-\Delta^{\rm per}+m^{2})^{-1+\kappa}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(x)H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(y)\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}(x,y)],

(7.69)

where
\mathcal{E}(x,y):=W_{T}(x)\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}(x)W_{T}(y)\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}(y)
. Notice that

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}(x,y)]\\
=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int\int\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,z)\dot{C}_{s}^{M}(y,z^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\left[W_{T}(x)W_{T}(y)\mathbb{W}_{t}^{3}(z)\mathbb{W}_{s}^{3}(z^{\prime})\right]dzdz^{\prime}dtds.

(7.70)

By Wick’s theorem, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[W_{T}(x)W_{T}(y)\mathbb{W}_{t}^{3}(z)\mathbb{W}_{t}^{3}(z^{\prime})\right]=\mathcal{S}_{1}(x,y,z,z^{\prime})+\mathcal{S}_{2}(x,y,z,z^{\prime})+\mathcal{S}_{3}(x,y,z,z^{\prime}),

(7.71)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}(x,y,z,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=6C_{T}^{M}(x,y)C_{t\wedge s}^{M}(z,z^{\prime})^{3},

(7.72)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}(x,y,z,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=18C_{t}^{M}(x,z)C_{t\wedge s}^{M}(z,z^{\prime})^{2}C_{s}^{M}(y,z^{\prime}),

(7.73)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3}(x,y,z,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=18C_{s}^{M}(x,z^{\prime})C_{t\wedge s}^{M}(z,z^{\prime})^{2}C_{t}^{M}(y,z).

(7.74)

For each
i
, define

\mathcal{S}_{i}^{\prime}(x,y):=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int\int\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,z)\dot{C}_{s}^{M}(y,z^{\prime})\mathcal{S}_{i}dzdz^{\prime}dtds.

(7.75)

By the interpolation estimates on the covariances, see Propositions
B.1
and
B.2
, and standard convolution bounds, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime}(x,y)

(7.76)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int\int\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{1+2\delta}\langle s\rangle^{1+2\delta}|x-z|^{1+2\delta}|y-z^{\prime}|^{1+2\delta}}

(7.77)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\frac{1}{|x-y|}\frac{(t\wedge s)^{\delta}\log\left(1+\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{1})^{2}}\right)}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{3-\delta}}dzdz^{\prime}dsdt

(7.78)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\frac{1}{|x-y|}\int\frac{1}{|x-z|^{1+2\delta}|y-z|^{1+\delta}}dz\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}}{|x-y|}

(7.79)

For the second term, integrating out the
t
and
s
variables and
z^{\prime}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int\int\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{1+2\delta}\langle s\rangle^{1+2\delta}|x-z|^{2+2\delta}|y-z^{\prime}|^{2+2\delta}}\frac{1}{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}dzdz^{\prime}dsdt

(7.80)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\frac{1}{|x-z|^{2+2\delta}|y-z|^{1+2\delta}}dz\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}}{|x-y|^{4\delta}}.

(7.81)

By symmetry, we also have

\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime}(x,y)\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}}{|x-y|^{4\delta}}.

(7.82)

Putting these together and using standard bounds on the Bessel potential kernel, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}\mathbb{W}_{T}^{[3]}\|^{2}_{H^{-1+\kappa}}\right]

(7.83)

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\frac{|(-\Delta+m^{2})^{-1+\kappa}(x,y)|}{|x-y|}|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(x)||H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(y)|dxdy

(7.84)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\int\int\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2+2\kappa}}|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(x)||H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(y)|\leq C^{\prime\prime}\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}\|_{L^{8/5}}^{2}

(7.85)

where the last inequality comes from using Young’s convolution inequality (duality form) with exponents
(p,q)
such that
2/p+1/q=2
(in this case, we choose
q=4/3
and
p=8/5
).

We now estimate the second term in
R_{1}(a)
. By a paraproduct decomposition in the
z
-variable,

\displaystyle W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}

\displaystyle=W_{T}\succ_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}+W_{T}\preccurlyeq_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}

(7.86)

We will need the following preliminary estimates on these terms. For the first term, by paraproduct estimates, for any
\kappa>0
, there exists
C=C(\kappa)>0
such that

\sup_{\tau}\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1/2+2\kappa}W_{T}\succ_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}\\
\leq C\sup_{\tau}\|W_{T}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\delta}}\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1/2+2\kappa}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L_{z}^{\infty}}.

(7.87)

For the second term, by para- and resonance product estimates and similar symmetry reasons,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau}

\displaystyle\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1+2\delta}(W_{T}\preccurlyeq_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(\tau,\cdot))\|_{L_{z}^{\infty}}

(7.88)

\displaystyle\leq C\|W_{T}\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}{\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1+2\kappa}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{1/2+\kappa}}}.

(7.89)

By using the paraproduct decomposition to decompose the second term in
R_{1}(a)
and inserting weight factors, we may write

\int W_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})K_{T}^{2}=R_{1}(a,2,1)+R_{1}(a,2,2),

(7.90)

where

\displaystyle R_{1}(a,2,1)

\displaystyle:=\int d(x,\partial M)^{1/2+2\kappa}(W_{T}\succ_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})(d(x,\partial M)^{-1/4-\kappa}K_{T})^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x,

(7.91)

\displaystyle R_{1}(a,2,2)

\displaystyle:=\int d(x,\partial M)^{1+2\kappa}(W_{T}\preccurlyeq_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})(d(x,\partial M)^{-1/2-\kappa}K_{T})^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(7.92)

By duality, the fractional Leibniz rule, and the preliminary estimate

\displaystyle|R_{1}(a,2,1)|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{\tau}\|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1/2+2\delta}W_{T}\succ_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}

(7.93)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\|(d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-1/4-\kappa}K_{T})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\tau}H^{1/2+\kappa}_{z}}.

(7.94)

By similar reasoning,

\displaystyle|R_{1}(a,2,2)|

\displaystyle\leq C\|d(\cdot,\partial M)^{1+2\kappa}W_{T}\preccurlyeq_{z}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-1/2-\delta}K_{T})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(7.95)

Observe that by Lemma
7.5
,

\max(\|(d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-1/4-\kappa}K_{T})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\tau}H^{1/2+\kappa}_{z}},\|(d(\cdot,\partial M)^{-1/2-\delta}K_{T})\|^{2}_{L^{2}})\leq\|K_{T}\|_{H^{3/4+\kappa}}^{2}.

(7.96)

The rest of the lemma now follows by a straightforward interpolation argument and we omit the details.

∎

7.4
Estimate of
R_{3}

Recall that

\displaystyle R_{3}=

\displaystyle\int_{M}\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}^{4}\rrbracket-\llbracket\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})_{T}^{4}\rrbracket-\llbracket\bar{H}(\varphi_{+})_{T}^{4}\rrbracket{\mathrm{d}}x

(7.97)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{M\Delta\{[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\}}\llbracket\overline{H}(\varphi_{-})_{T}^{4}\rrbracket+\llbracket\overline{H}(\varphi_{+})_{T}^{4}\rrbracket dx.

(7.98)

Lemma 7.8
.

For every
\kappa>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C>0
such that for every
T>0
and
\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}\in\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa}_{z}
,

|R_{3}|\leq\sum_{\sigma\in\{\pm\}}C\Big(\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa}_{z}}^{4}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sup_{\tau\in M}|d(\tau,\partial M)^{1-\kappa}\|\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{\sigma}))^{i}\rrbracket(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\kappa/2}_{z}}|\Big)

(7.99)

Proof.

Let us first observe that the bound on the integral supported on
M\Delta\{[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\}
is straightforward to estimate, therefore we only estimate the first integral. We will drop the subscript
T
for convenience.

Note that we can write

H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})=\overline{H}(\varphi_{-})+\overline{H}(\varphi_{+})+S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}),

(7.100)

where
S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})
is
m
-harmonic with boundary data
\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})|_{\partial^{+}M}
and
\bar{H}(\varphi_{+})|_{\partial^{-}M}
, and therefore
S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})
is smooth up to the boundary. Using this decomposition, we may write

\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4}\rrbracket\\
=\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\binom{4}{i}\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})+\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{i}\rrbracket\cdot S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4-i}+\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})+\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4}\rrbracket.

(7.101)

We begin by estimating

\int\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})+\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4}\rrbracket-\llbracket\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})^{4}\rrbracket-\llbracket\bar{H}(\varphi_{+})^{4}\rrbracket.

(7.102)

By the Binomial theorem for Wick powers,

\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})+\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4}\rrbracket-\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{4}\rrbracket-\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4}\rrbracket\\
=\sum_{1\leq j\leq 3}\binom{4}{j}\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{j}\rrbracket\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4-j}\rrbracket.

(7.103)

We now estimate the integrals above for each
j\in\{1,2,3\}
separately. Let
c>0
be sufficiently small and write
M=M^{\rm bulk}(c)\sqcup M^{\rm bdry}(c)
, where
{\rm dist}(M^{\rm bulk}(c),\partial M)\geq c
. Then we split the integral to bulk and boundary contributions as follows:

\int\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{j}\rrbracket\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4-j}\rrbracket{\mathrm{d}}x\\
\\
=\int_{M^{\rm bulk}(c)}\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{i}\rrbracket\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4-i}\rrbracket{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{M^{\rm bdry}(c)}\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{i}\rrbracket\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4-i}\rrbracket{\mathrm{d}}x.

(7.104)

The first integral, corresponding to the bulk contribution, is clearly bounded by
\|\varphi_{-}\|^{4}_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}+\|\varphi_{+}\|^{4}_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}
due to
the smoothing of the harmonic extension and Hölder’s inequality. For the second term – the boundary contribution – it is convenient to split it further to
M^{\rm bdry}_{\pm}(c)
according to which boundary component is nearest. Without loss of generality, we estimate
M^{\rm bdry}_{-}(c)
. By duality and smoothing properties of the
m
-harmonic extension, there exists
C=C(c)>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{M^{\rm bdry}_{-}(c)}\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{j}\rrbracket\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4-j}\rrbracket\right|

(7.105)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in M^{\rm bdry}_{-}(c)}\|\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{4-j}(\tau,\cdot)\rrbracket\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa/2}_{z}}

(7.106)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\int\frac{d(\tau,\partial M_{-})^{1-\kappa}}{d(\tau,\partial M_{-})^{1-\kappa}}\|\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{j}\rrbracket(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\kappa/2}_{z}}d\tau

(7.107)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\varphi_{-}\|^{4-j}_{\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}\sup_{\tau\in M^{\rm bdry}_{-}(c)}|d(\tau,\partial M_{-})^{1-\kappa}\|\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{j}\rrbracket(\tau,\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\kappa/2}_{z}}|.

(7.108)

We now must estimate the integrals corresponding to the terms
i\in\{0,1,2,3\}
in (
7.101
), that is

\int\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\binom{4}{i}\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})+\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{i}\rrbracket S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4-i}

(7.109)

In the case
i=0
, we use the fact
that by elliptic regularity theory and smoothing properties of
\bar{H}
,

\|S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|_{C^{2}}\leq C(\|\varphi_{-}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}+\|\varphi_{+}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}).

(7.110)

Hence
14
14

14

Note that in this case, the desired estimate simply follows from the maximum principle. We will use the stronger regularity estimate below.
,

\left|\int S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4}\right|\leq C^{\prime}{\rm max}\left(\|\varphi_{-}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}},\|\varphi_{+}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\delta}_{z}}\right)^{4}.

(7.111)

For the other terms, we expand further

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}\binom{4}{i}

\displaystyle\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-})+\bar{H}(\varphi_{+}))^{i}\rrbracket S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4-i}

(7.112)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\binom{4}{i}\binom{i}{k}\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{k}\rrbracket\llbracket\bar{H}(\varphi_{+})^{i-k}\rrbracket S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4-i}

(7.113)

We will estimate this integrals in a unified way. Let
c>0
be sufficiently small and
M^{\rm bulk}(c)
,
M^{\rm bdry}_{-}(c)
and
M^{\rm bdry}_{+}(c)
be as above. We split the integrand into boundary and bulk contributions in the obvious way:

\displaystyle\int\llbracket(\bar{H}(\varphi_{-}))^{k}\rrbracket\llbracket\bar{H}(\varphi_{+})^{i-k}\rrbracket S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4-i}=I^{\rm bulk}+I^{\rm bdry}_{-}+I^{\rm bdry}_{+}.

(7.114)

The desired estimate on
I^{\rm bulk}
is trivial because all terms are smooth. By symmetry, we only consider
I^{\rm bdry}_{-}
. By duality

\displaystyle|I^{\rm bdry}_{-}|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in M^{\rm bdry}_{-}(c)}\|\llbracket\bar{H}(\varphi_{+})^{i-k}\rrbracket S(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{4-i}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H^{1-\kappa}_{z}}

(7.115)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{M_{-}^{\rm bdry}(c)}\frac{d(\tau,\partial M_{-})^{1-\kappa}}{d(\tau,\partial M_{-})^{1-\kappa}}\|\llbracket\overline{H}(\varphi_{-})^{k}\rrbracket\|_{H^{-1+\kappa}_{z}}d\tau

(7.116)

\displaystyle\leq C{\rm max}(\|\varphi_{-}\|^{4-k}_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-2\kappa}_{z}},\|\varphi_{+}\|^{4-k}_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-2\kappa}_{z}})

(7.117)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sup_{\tau\in M^{\rm bdry}_{-}(c)}|d(\tau,\partial M_{-})^{1-\kappa}\|\llbracket\overline{H}(\varphi_{-})^{k}\rrbracket\|_{H^{-1+\kappa}_{z}}|.

(7.118)

The desired estimate follows.
∎

7.5
Estimate of
R_{4}

We write
R_{4}=R_{4}(a)+R_{4}(b)
, where

\displaystyle R_{4}(a)

\displaystyle=-\int_{M}(\gamma_{T}-\gamma_{T}^{M})\Big(2W_{T}Z_{T}+\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}_{T}\rrbracket+2H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}Z_{T}+Z_{T}^{2}\Big)\,dx,

(7.119)

\displaystyle R_{4}(b)

\displaystyle=-\delta_{T}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})+\Delta\delta^{0}_{T}+\delta^{M}_{T}.

(7.120)

In order to estimate
R_{4}(a)
, we begin with a preliminary estimate on the function
\gamma_{T}-\gamma_{T}^{M}
. Recall that
C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}
is the covariance of the massive GFF on
M
with periodic boundary conditions and that

\displaystyle\gamma_{T}^{M}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3}dy,

(7.121)

\displaystyle\gamma_{T}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\int C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(x,y)^{3}dy.

(7.122)

Lemma 7.9
.

For every
p\in[1,\infty)
, there exists
C=C(p)>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{T}-\gamma_{T}^{M}\|_{L^{p}}\leq C.

(7.123)

Proof.

It is sufficient to show that for every
\kappa>0
, there exists
C=C(\kappa)>0
such that for every
x\in M
,

|\gamma_{T}(x)-\gamma_{T}^{M}(x)|\leq Cd(x,\partial M)^{-\kappa}.

(7.124)

Recall that from the Markov property (see Lemma
3.2
),
C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(x,y)=C_{T}^{B}(x,y)+C_{T}^{M}(x,y)
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\int C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(x,y)^{3}{\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}\binom{3}{i}\int C_{T}^{B}(x,y)^{i}C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3-i}{\mathrm{d}}y-\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3}{\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\cdot 12^{2}}{3}\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}\binom{3}{i}\int C_{T}^{B}(x,y)^{i}C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3-i}{\mathrm{d}}y+\gamma_{T}^{M}(x).

We now use the kernel estimates on
C_{T}^{M}
and
C_{T}^{B}
contained in Appendices
B
and
C
to obtain the following. For every
\kappa>0
, there exists
C=C(\kappa)>0
such that for every
i\in\{1,2,3\}
,

\left|\int C_{T}^{B}(x,y)^{i}C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3-i}dy\right|\\
\leq C\left|\int\frac{1}{|x-y|^{3-i}}\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{-\kappa}}{|x-y|^{i-\kappa}}dy\right|\leq C^{\prime}d(x,\partial M)^{-\kappa}.

(7.125)

The desired estimate follows.
∎

We now estimate
R_{4}(a)
.

Lemma 7.10
.

For every
\delta>0
, there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|R_{4}(a)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|^{2}_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|\llbracket(H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}))^{2}\rrbracket\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\tau}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1-\kappa}}\right)

(7.126)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\delta\left(\|K_{T}\|^{2}_{H^{1-\kappa}}+\|Z_{T}\|^{4}_{L^{4}}\right).

(7.127)

Proof.

By the intermediate ansatz (
5.43
),

\displaystyle R_{4}(a)

\displaystyle\equiv-\int(\gamma_{T}-\gamma_{T}^{M})\Big(2W_{T}K_{T}+\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}\rrbracket+2H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})K_{T}+Z_{T}^{2}\Big).

(7.128)

Note that the last term can be bounded by the Cauchy-Schwarz and Young inequalities: for every
\delta>0
, there exists
C>0
such that

\left|\int(\gamma_{T}-\gamma_{T}^{M})Z_{T}^{2}\right|\leq\|\gamma_{T}-\gamma_{T}^{M}\|_{L^{2}}\|Z_{T}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\leq C\|\gamma_{T}-\gamma_{T}^{M}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\delta\|Z_{T}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}.

(7.129)

The required estimate follows from Lemma
7.9
.

For the remaining terms, we will split
\tau
-slices. Note that by translation invariance in the axial (z) direction, we have that

(\gamma_{T}-\gamma^{M}_{T})(x)=(\gamma_{T}-\gamma^{M}_{T})(\tau).

Therefore, for every
\delta>0
, there exists
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Big|\int(\gamma_{T}-\gamma^{M}_{T})(W_{T}+H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}))K_{T}dx\Big|

(7.130)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|W_{T}\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}})\int|(\gamma_{T}-\gamma^{M}_{T})(\tau)|\|K_{T}(\tau,\cdot)\|_{H_{z}^{1-\kappa}}d\tau

(7.131)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\left(\|(\gamma_{T}-\gamma^{M}_{T})\|^{4}_{L_{\tau}^{2}}+\|W_{T}\|^{4}_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|^{4}_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)+\delta\|K_{T}\|^{2}_{L_{\tau}^{2}H_{z}^{1-\kappa}}.

(7.132)

We then use (
7.54
) to conclude.

For the remaining term, by splitting the integral, using duality and uniformly bounding the integral, we obtain

\left|\int(\gamma_{T}-\gamma^{M}_{T})\llbracket(H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}))^{2}\rrbracket{\mathrm{d}}x\right|\leq C\|\gamma_{T}-\gamma^{M}_{T}\|^{2}_{L_{\tau}^{2}}+\delta\|\llbracket(H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}))^{2}\rrbracket\|^{2}_{L_{\tau}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{z}^{-1-\kappa}}.

(7.133)

∎

We now turn to the energy renormalization terms
R_{4}(b)
. The term
\Delta\delta^{0}_{T}
is bounded by the following lemma.

Lemma 7.11
.

We have that

\delta^{0}_{T}=-2\cdot 4!\int\int C_{T}^{B}(x,y)^{4}dxdy+O(1).

(7.134)

Proof.

Note that we may replace the integral over
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
by an integral over
M
up to an
O(1)
term by smoothing properties of the harmonic extension
\bar{H}
. Let us compute

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\left(\int\llbracket\bar{H}\varphi_{T}(x)^{4}\rrbracket dx\right)^{2}-\left(\int\llbracket H\varphi_{T}(x)^{4}\rrbracket dx\right)^{2}

(7.135)

\displaystyle=\left(\int\llbracket\overline{H}\varphi_{T}(x)^{4}\rrbracket-\llbracket H\varphi_{T}(x)^{4}\rrbracket\right)\left(\int\llbracket\overline{H}\varphi_{T}(x)^{4}\rrbracket+\llbracket H\varphi_{T}(x)^{4}\rrbracket\right)

(7.136)

\displaystyle=E_{1}\times E_{2}.

(7.137)

Writing
H\varphi_{T}=\bar{H}\varphi_{T}+S\varphi_{T}
, by the Binomial theorem for Wick powers we have

E_{1}=\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int\binom{4}{i}\llbracket\overline{H}\varphi_{T}(x)^{i}\rrbracket(S\varphi_{T})^{4-i}.

(7.138)

By Wick’s theorem,

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}

\displaystyle\binom{4}{i}\int\int\mathbb{E}\left[\llbracket\overline{H}\varphi_{T}(x)^{i}\rrbracket(S\varphi_{T})^{4-i}\llbracket\overline{H}\varphi_{T}(x^{\prime})^{4}\rrbracket\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\llbracket\overline{H}\varphi_{T}(x)^{i}\rrbracket(S\varphi_{T})^{4-i}\llbracket H\varphi_{T}(x^{\prime})^{4}\rrbracket\right]

(7.139)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}4!\binom{4}{i}\int\int\overline{C}_{T}^{B}(x,x^{\prime})^{i}\bar{K}^{S}_{T}(x,x^{\prime})^{4-i}+K_{T}^{H,\bar{H}}(x,x^{\prime})^{i}K_{T}^{S}(x,x^{\prime})^{4-i},

(7.140)

where

\displaystyle\bar{K}^{S}_{T}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[S\varphi_{T}(x)\bar{H}\varphi_{T}(x^{\prime})],

(7.141)

\displaystyle K^{S}_{T}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[S\varphi_{T}(x)H\varphi_{T}(x^{\prime})],

(7.142)

\displaystyle K_{T}^{H,\overline{H}}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[H\varphi_{T}(x)\overline{H}\varphi_{T}(x^{\prime})].

(7.143)

We claim that there exists
C>0
such that for every
T>0
,

\sup_{x,x^{\prime}}{\rm max}\left(K_{T}^{S}(x,x^{\prime}),\bar{K}_{T}^{S}(x,x^{\prime})\right)\leq C.

(7.144)

The lemma then follows since the
(C_{T}^{B})^{i}
and
(K_{T}^{H,\bar{H}})^{i}
are locally integrable for
i=1,2,3
. See the covariance estimates in Appendix
C
.

We now prove the claim. Without loss of generality, let us consider
K_{T}^{S}
. Writing in terms of the convolution kernels, we have that

K_{T}^{S}(x,x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}S(x,y)P(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})C_{T}^{0}(y,y^{\prime})dydy^{\prime}.

(7.145)

The term
S(x,y)
is smooth. The Poisson kernel is of order
P(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\asymp\frac{1}{|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}
. The kernel of
(-\Delta_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}+m^{2})^{-1}
is of order
C_{T}^{0}(y,y^{\prime})\asymp\frac{1}{|y-y^{\prime}|}
. By these considerations together with translation invariance,

|K_{T}^{S}(x,x^{\prime})|\leq C\|S\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\frac{1}{|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\frac{1}{|y-y^{\prime}|}dydy^{\prime}\leq C^{\prime}.

(7.146)

∎

It remains to estimate
\delta_{T}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})-\delta_{T}^{M}
. Recall that

\delta_{T}^{M}:=\sum_{i=1}^{5}\delta_{T}^{i,M},

(7.147)

where

\displaystyle\delta_{T}^{1,M}

\displaystyle:=-12\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{4}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}y,

(7.148)

\displaystyle\delta_{T}^{2,M}

\displaystyle:=6\cdot 4^{2}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 2\int\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{2}C_{T}^{M}(y,y^{\prime})^{2}C_{T}^{M}(y^{\prime},x)^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}y^{\prime},

(7.149)

\displaystyle\delta_{T}^{3,M}

\displaystyle:=\frac{12^{2}}{3}\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3}\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{0}\otimes\mu^{0}}\left[H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(x)H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(y)\right]{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}y,

(7.150)

\displaystyle\delta^{4,M}_{T}

\displaystyle:=\frac{12^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{0}\otimes\mu^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int J_{t}(\mathbb{W}_{t}\,\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{2}\rrbracket)^{2}dxdt\right],

(7.151)

\displaystyle\delta^{5,M}_{T}

\displaystyle:=\frac{4^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{0}\otimes\mu^{0}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int J_{t}(\llbracket H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})^{3}\rrbracket)^{2}dxdt\right].

(7.152)

We may rewrite
\delta^{3,M}_{T},\delta^{4,M}_{T}
, and
\delta^{5,M}_{T}
in terms of kernels as follows:

\displaystyle\delta^{3,M}_{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{12^{2}}{3}\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{3}C_{T}^{B}(x,y){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}y,

(7.153)

\displaystyle\delta^{4,M}_{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{12^{2}}{2}\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{2}C_{T}^{B}(x,y)^{2}dxdy,

(7.154)

\displaystyle\delta^{5,M}_{T}

\displaystyle=3!\cdot\frac{4^{2}}{2}\int\int C_{T}^{M}(x,y)C_{T}^{B}(x,y)^{3}dxdy.

(7.155)

By Remark
3.5
(or direct inspection), it thus follows that

|\delta_{T}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})-\delta_{T}^{M}|\\
\leq C\int\int\int\left|C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(x,y)^{2}C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(y,y^{\prime})^{2}C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(y^{\prime},x)^{2}-C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{2}C_{T}^{M}(y,y^{\prime})^{2}C_{T}^{M}(y^{\prime},x)^{2}\right|.

(7.156)

Lemma 7.12
.

There exists
C>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

|\delta_{T}(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})-\delta_{T}^{M}|\leq C.

(7.157)

Proof.

As explained above, it suffices to show that

\int\int\int\left|C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(x,y)^{2}C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(y,y^{\prime})^{2}C_{T}^{M^{\rm per}}(y^{\prime},x)^{2}-C_{T}^{M}(x,y)^{2}C_{T}^{M}(y,y^{\prime})^{2}C_{T}^{M}(y^{\prime},x)^{2}\right|\leq C.

(7.158)

Since
C^{M^{\rm per}}_{T}(x,y)=C_{T}^{M}(x,y)+C_{T}^{B}(x,y)
, it is sufficient to show that there exists
C>0
such that for every
1\leq i\leq 2
and
0\leq j,k\leq 2
, and every
T>0
,

\int\int\int\left|{C^{M}_{T}}(x,y)^{2-i}{C^{B}_{T}}(x,y)^{i}C_{T}^{M}\left(y,y^{\prime}\right)^{2-j}C_{T}^{B}\left(y,y^{\prime}\right)^{j}C_{T}^{M}(x,y^{\prime})^{2-k}C_{T}^{M}(x,y^{\prime})^{k}\right|\leq C.

(7.159)

In order to establish this, recall the pointwise covariance estimates of Appendices
B
and
C
:

|C_{T}^{M}(x,y)|,|C_{T}^{B}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C}{|x-y|}.

(7.160)

Furthermore, by the covariance interpolation estimates (see Appendix
C
), for every
0<\alpha<1
we have

|C^{B}_{T}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{1}{|x-y|^{1-\alpha}}\frac{1}{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}.

(7.161)

Thus we get for any
1\leq i\leq 2
and
0\leq j,k\leq 2
,

\displaystyle\int\int\int

\displaystyle\left|{C^{M}_{T}}(x,y)^{2-i}{C^{B}_{T}}(x,y)^{i}C_{T}^{M}\left(y,y^{\prime}\right)^{2-j}C_{T}^{B}\left(y,y^{\prime}\right)^{j}C_{T}^{M}(x,y^{\prime})^{2-k}C_{T}^{M}(x,y^{\prime})^{k}\right|\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(7.162)

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\int\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2-\alpha}}\frac{1}{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}\frac{1}{|y-y^{\prime}|^{2}}\frac{1}{|x-y^{\prime}|^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(7.163)

\displaystyle\leq C\int\int\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2-\alpha}}\frac{1}{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}\frac{1}{|x-y|}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\leq\int\frac{1}{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}x\leq C.

(7.164)

∎

7.6
Estimate of
R_{8}

Recall that

R_{8}=\int^{T}_{0}\int(J_{t}(\mathbb{W}^{2}_{t}\succ Z^{\flat}_{t}))(J_{t}(\mathbb{W}^{2}_{t}\,H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T})){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t-\int 2\gamma^{M}_{T}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})_{T}Z_{T}{\mathrm{d}}x.

(7.165)

We will prove the following estimate on
R_{8}
.

Lemma 7.13
.

For every
\delta>0
sufficiently small, there exists
C_{\delta}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|R_{8}|]\leq C_{\delta}(1+\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|_{B^{s(p)}_{p,p}}^{q(p)})+\delta\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{C}_{T}(u)],

(7.166)

where
p\in[1,2)
is sufficiently close to
2
,
s(p)
is sufficiently small, and is as below and
q(p)\in[1,\infty)
is sufficiently large.

As a first step, we prove a regularity estimate on
H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})
measured in a Besov norm of regularity
s>0
taken sufficiently small. The difficulty comes from the discontinuity at the image of
\partial M_{-}
and
\partial M_{+}
under the identification.

Lemma 7.14
.

Let
\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+}\in\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa}_{z}
. Then for every
p\in[1,2)
, there exists
s>0
sufficiently small and
C>0
such that

\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|_{B^{s}_{p,p}}\leq C(1+\|\varphi_{-}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa}_{z}}+\|\varphi_{+}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-1/2-\kappa}_{z}}).

(7.167)

Proof.

Note that
B^{s}_{p,p,\tau}B^{s}_{p,p,z}\hookrightarrow B^{s}_{p,p}
. Thus, by this and standard embeddings, it is sufficient to establish the above bound for
W^{s}_{p,\tau}B^{s}_{p,p,z}
. Recall that

\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|_{W^{s}_{p,\tau}B^{s}_{p,p,z}}^{p}=\int_{L\mathbb{T}}\int_{L\mathbb{T}}\frac{\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau,\cdot)-H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau^{\prime},\cdot)\|^{p}_{B^{s}_{p,p,z}}}{|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|^{d+sp}}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}=:I.

(7.168)

We may assume that we shift by
+L
so that
H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})
is continuous, in fact smooth, (in the appropriate space) except possibly at
\tau=0
. It is therefore sufficient to estimate this integral on the set
\Sigma:=(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)^{2}
.

We first estimate the integral
I
on the set
\Sigma_{1}:=\{\tau<\tau^{\prime}<0:\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in\Sigma\}
. Let
\delta>0
be sufficiently small. Then

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Sigma_{1}}\frac{\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau,\cdot)-H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau^{\prime},\cdot)\|^{p}_{B^{s}_{p,p,z}}}{|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|^{1+sp}}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}

(7.169)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Sigma_{1}}\frac{\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau,\cdot)-H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau^{\prime},\cdot)\|^{p(1-\delta)}_{B^{s}_{p,p,z}}}{|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|^{1+sp}}

(7.170)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\int_{\tau}^{\tau^{\prime}}\|\partial_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau^{\prime\prime},\cdot)\|_{B^{s}_{p,p,z}}d\tau^{\prime\prime}\right)^{p\delta}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}.

(7.171)

By properties of the harmonic extension (see Appendix
C
), we have that

\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{s}_{p,p,z}}\leq c_{1}\tau^{-1/2-\kappa-s}\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}B^{-1/2-\kappa}_{p,p,z}}.

(7.172)

Thus, we may bound

\displaystyle I_{1}
