Proposition B.1
.

Let
\alpha\in[0,1)
. Then there exists
C>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

|C^{M}_{T}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{\langle T\rangle^{\alpha}}{|x-y|^{1-\alpha}},\qquad\forall x,y\in M.

(B.1)

In the case
\alpha>0
, Proposition
B.1
follows from Proposition
B.2
and the fundamental theorem of calculus. The case
\alpha=0
uses the fact that
C^{M}_{T}(x,y)=\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{2}\ast C^{M}_{\infty}(x,y)dt
and standard bounds on convolutions and the usual Dirichlet Green function
C^{M}_{\infty}
.

B.2
Interpolation of time-derivative with cutoff scale
T

Proposition B.2
.

Let
\alpha\in[0,1)
. Then there exists
C>0
such that, for every
t>0
,

|\dot{C}^{M}_{t}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-1}}{|x-y|^{1-\alpha}},\qquad\forall x,y\in M.

(B.2)

Furthermore, in the case
\alpha\in(-1,0)
, there exists
C>0
such that for every
t>0
,

|\dot{C}^{M}_{t}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{\langle t\rangle^{\alpha-1}\log\left(1+\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{(x_{1}-y_{1})^{2}}\right)}{|x-y|^{1-\alpha}},\qquad\forall x,y\in M.

(B.3)

Proof.

First of all, note that

\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\delta_{u_{1}=y_{1}}\otimes\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{2})(u-y)C_{\infty}^{M}(u,x)du.

(B.4)

Here we abuse notation, denoting by
\rho
the inverse Fourier Transform fo
\rho
. This is consistent with denoting operators and kernels in the same way.
Note that
\delta_{u_{1}=y_{1}}\otimes\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{2}(u-y)=\delta_{u_{1}=y_{1}}\langle t\rangle^{-1}\langle t\rangle^{2}\rho^{\prime}(\langle t\rangle((u_{2},u_{3})-(y_{2},y_{3})))
. By abuse of notation, let us write this as
\langle t\rangle\chi(\langle t\rangle(u-y))
with (vector in the
(e_{2},e_{3})
coordinates) primitive
\tilde{\chi}(\langle t\rangle(u-y))
— we will use the primitive when considering the case
\alpha<0
below.

Let us first analyze when
\alpha>0
. By the estimate on the Green function (see
[
CKS10
]
),

\displaystyle|\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\langle t\rangle^{2}|\chi(\langle t\rangle(u-y))|\frac{1}{|u-x|}du

(B.5)

\displaystyle=\langle t\rangle^{-1+2-\beta}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\beta}|u-y|^{\beta}|\chi(\langle t\rangle(u-y))|\frac{1}{|u-y|^{\beta}|u-x|}du.

(B.6)

Choosing
1<\beta<2
and computing the convolution, we thus have that

|\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,y)|\leq c_{2}\frac{\langle t\rangle^{1-\beta}}{|x-y|^{\beta-1}}.

(B.7)

Choosing
\beta=2-\alpha
then yields the desired estimate in the case
\alpha\in(0,1)
.

We now consider
\alpha=0
. We split
\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,y)=I_{1}+I_{2}
according to whether
|u-y|\geq|x-y|/2
or
|u-y|\leq|x-y|/2
, respectively. In the first case, we can proceed above to obtain

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq c_{3}\frac{\langle t\rangle^{-1}}{|x-y|^{\varepsilon}}\int_{|u-y|\leq|x-y|/2}\frac{1}{|u-y|^{2-\varepsilon}|u-x|}\leq c_{4}\frac{\langle t\rangle^{-1}}{|x-y|}.

(B.8)

On the other hand, when
|u-y|\leq|x-y|/2
, we have that
|u-x|\geq|x-y|/2
. Hence

|I_{2}|\leq c_{5}\frac{\langle t\rangle^{-1}}{|x-y|}\int\langle t\rangle^{2}|\chi(\langle t\rangle(u-y))|du\leq c_{6}\frac{\langle t\rangle^{-1}}{|x-y|}.

(B.9)

We will now analyze the case when
\alpha<0
in which we will obtain a log correction. By integration by parts, and recalling the primitive
\tilde{\chi}
, we have that

\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\tilde{\chi}(\langle t\rangle(u-y))\cdot\nabla_{(u_{2},u_{3})}C^{M}_{\infty}(u,x)du.

(B.10)

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
be arbitrary. Then by the gradient estimate on the Green function (see for instance
[
CKS10
]
),

\displaystyle|\dot{C}_{t}^{M}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-\beta}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\delta_{u_{1}=y_{1}}\langle t\rangle^{\beta}|u-y|^{\beta}|\tilde{\chi}(\langle t\rangle(u-y))|\frac{1}{|u-y|^{\beta}|u-x|^{2}}du

(B.11)

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\langle t\rangle^{-\beta}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\frac{1}{((u_{2}-y_{2})^{2}+(u_{3}-y_{3})^{2})^{\beta/2}}

(B.12)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\frac{1}{((y_{1}-x_{1})^{2}+(u_{2}-x_{2})^{2}+(u_{3}-x_{3})^{2})^{2}}du

(B.13)

provided
0<\beta<2
. We claim that the convolution above is finite and in fact bounded by

c_{3}\frac{\langle t\rangle^{-\beta}\log\left(1+\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{(x_{1}-y_{1})^{2}}\right)}{|x-y|^{\beta}}.

(B.14)

Setting
\beta=1-\alpha
then yields the desired estimate with the range
\alpha\in(-1,0)
(and in fact a suboptimal estimate in the full range
\alpha\in(-1,1)
).

We now turn to the convolution and for ease of notation set
y=0
(the other cases follow similarly). Then we need to estimate

I:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\frac{1}{(u_{2}^{2}+u_{3}^{2})^{\beta/2}(x_{1}^{2}+(u_{2}-x_{2})^{2}+(u_{3}-x_{3})^{2}}du.

(B.15)

Let us split it into the integration domains:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\{x_{1}^{2}+(u_{2}-x_{2})^{2}+(u_{3}-x_{3})^{2}\leq|x|^{2}/4\},\,\mathcal{A}_{2}:=\mathbb{T}^{2}\setminus\mathcal{A}_{1}.

(B.16)

On
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, we have that
c|x|\leq|(u_{2},u_{3})|\leq C|x|
. Thus we obtain that

I(\mathcal{A}_{1})\leq\frac{c_{3}}{|x|^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{|x|}\frac{r}{(x_{1}^{2}+r^{2})}dr=\frac{c_{3}}{2|x|^{\beta}}(\log(x_{1}^{2}+|x|^{2})-\log(x_{1}^{2}))

(B.17)

On
\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c}
, we have that

I(\mathcal{A}_{2})\leq\frac{c_{4}}{|x|^{\varepsilon}}\int\frac{1}{|(u_{2},u_{3})|^{\beta}|(0,u_{2},u_{3})-x|^{2-\varepsilon}}du\leq\frac{c_{5}}{|x|^{\beta}}.

(B.18)

∎

B.3
Interpolation with distance to boundary

Proposition B.3
.

Let
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in[0,1)
. Then there exists
C>0
such that, for every
T>0
,

|C^{M}_{T}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha_{1}}d(y,\partial M)^{\alpha_{2}}}{|x-y|^{1+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}},\qquad\forall x,y\in M.

(B.19)

Proof.

By symmetry and interpolation, it is sufficient to prove that for
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

|C^{M}_{T}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}{|x-y|^{1+\alpha}}.

(B.20)

We will use that for the case
T=\infty
,

|C^{M}_{\infty}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{\min(d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha},d(y,\partial M)^{\alpha})}{|x-y|^{1+\alpha}},

(B.21)

see for instance
[
CKS10
]
.

Note that

C_{T}^{M}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\delta_{u_{1}=y_{1}}\otimes\langle T\rangle^{2}\rho^{2}(\langle T\rangle(u-y))C^{M}_{T}(u,x)du.

(B.22)

Let us split this integral into
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
according to whether
|u-y|\geq|x-y|/2
or
|u-y|\leq|x-y|/2
, respectively.

For
I_{1}
, using the bound above,

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}\int\delta_{u_{1}=y_{1}}\otimes\langle T\rangle^{2}\rho^{2}(\langle T\rangle(u-y))|u-y|^{\beta}\frac{1}{|u-y|^{\beta}|u-x|^{1+\alpha}}du

(B.23)

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\langle T\rangle^{2-\beta}d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}\int\frac{1}{|u-y|^{\beta}|u-x|^{1+\alpha}}du

(B.24)

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}\frac{\langle T\rangle^{2-\beta}d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}{|x-y|^{\varepsilon}}\int\frac{1}{|u-y|^{\beta-\varepsilon}|u-x|^{1+\alpha}}du,

(B.25)

where we choose
\beta=2
. Hence,

|I_{1}|\leq c_{4}\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}{|x-y|^{1+\alpha}}.

(B.26)

Now, in the case
|u-y|\leq|x-y|/2
, we have that
|u-x|\geq|x-y|/2
. Hence by the bound on
C^{M}_{\infty}(x,y)
, we have that

|I_{2}|\leq c_{1}\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}{|x-y|^{1+\alpha}}\int\delta_{u_{1}=y_{1}}\otimes\langle T\rangle^{2}\rho^{2}(\langle T\rangle(u-y))du\leq c_{2}\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}{|x-y|^{1+\alpha}}.

(B.27)

Combining the estimates for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
yields the desired result.

∎

B.4
Properties of
J_{t}

Lemma B.4

(Commutator estimate)
.

For every
s<0
, every
p\in[1,\infty)
, Then for
1/q>1/p+2\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon<1/4

\|J_{t}(f\succ g)-J_{t}f\succ g\|_{B^{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{p,p}}\leq C\langle t\rangle^{-1/2-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{s-\varepsilon}}\|g\|_{B^{1/4}_{q,q}}.

(B.28)

Proof.

Let us write
j_{t}:=J_{t}^{\rm per}
, the corresponding map defined as in the periodic case. The commutator estimate for
j_{t}
follows, so therefore we have to estimate the terms

T_{1}:=(J_{t}-j_{t})(f\succ g),\qquad T_{2}:=((J_{t}-j_{t})f)\succ g.

(B.29)

We will analyze
T_{1}
, the desired estimate on
T_{2}
follows similarly.

We use the heat kernel decomposition:

J_{t}-j_{t}:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\rho_{t}^{2}}\right)\ast\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{-1/2}e^{s(\Delta-m^{2})}ds-\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{-1/2}e^{s(\Delta^{\rm per}-m^{2})}ds\right),

(B.30)

where we recall
\ast
is convolution in the
z
-direction.
The first factor satisfies the bound

\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\rho_{t}^{2}}\right)\ast f\|_{B^{s-\varepsilon}_{p,p}}\leq\langle t\rangle^{-1/2-\varepsilon}\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,p}}.

(B.31)

And we need to estimate the operator on the right. Let us define this operator to be
\Gamma
. The kernel of
\Gamma
viewed as an operator on
M^{\rm per}
is given by

\Gamma(x,y):=\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{-1/2}\left(e^{s(\Delta-m^{2})}(x,y)-e^{s(\Delta^{\rm per}-m^{2})}(x,y)\right)ds.

(B.32)

We represent the heat kernels by their Poisson summations. Let
(-\Delta^{\infty}+m^{2})
be the massive Laplacian on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Then (using the method of images for the second one), we have that

\displaystyle e^{s(\Delta^{\rm per}-m^{2})}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}e^{s(\Delta^{\infty}-m^{2})}(x+n,y),

(B.33)

\displaystyle e^{s(\Delta-m^{2})}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}(-1)^{n_{1}}e^{s(\Delta^{\infty}-m^{2})}(x+n,y).

(B.34)

Hence,

e^{s(\Delta-m^{2})}(x,y)-e^{s(\Delta^{\rm per}-m^{2})}(x,y)=-2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
n_{1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}+1\end{subarray}}e^{s(\Delta^{\infty}-m^{2})}(x+n,y).

(B.35)

We now claim that for
\beta
a multindex with
|\beta|\leq 2
for any
\alpha<2+|\beta|

|\partial_{x,y}^{\beta}\Gamma(x,y)|\leq\frac{{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}}}{|x-y|^{2+|\beta|-\alpha}}

(B.36)

We now split
\Gamma=\Gamma^{<}+\Gamma^{>}
according to the
s
integration near
0
and at
\infty
, respectively. Let us first estimate
\Gamma^{>}
. By the above, it suffices to estimate

\Gamma^{>}(x,y)=-2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
n_{1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}+1\end{subarray}}\int_{s_{0}}^{\infty}s^{-1/2}\frac{1}{(2\pi s)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{|x+n-y|^{2}}{2s}}e^{-sm^{2}}ds.

(B.37)

Thanks to the exponential decay in
s
due to the
m^{2}
term,
\Gamma^{>}
and its derivatives are uniformly bounded.

We now turn to
\Gamma^{<}
. We do the
\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{x}
derivative, the other follows similarly. From above, we have that

\displaystyle|\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{x}\Gamma^{<}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
n_{1}\in 2\mathbb{Z}+1\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{s_{0}}\frac{|x+n-y|}{s^{3}}e^{-\frac{|x+n-y|^{2}}{2s}}ds.

(B.38)

We split the sum according to when
n_{1}\in\{-1,1\}
and otherwise. For the first, we have that by a change of variables

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
n_{1}=-1,1\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{s_{0}}\frac{|x+n-y|}{s^{3}}e^{-\frac{|x+n-y|^{2}}{2s}}ds\\
\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
n_{1}=-1,1\end{subarray}}\frac{c_{2}}{|x+n-y|^{3}}\leq c_{3}\frac{\min(d(x,\partial M)^{-\delta},d(y,\partial M)^{-\delta})}{|x-y|^{3-\delta}}.

(B.39)

For the second, we use that
|x-n-y|\geq c_{4}|n_{1}|
uniformly over
x
and
y
. From this, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|n_{1}|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{s_{0}}\frac{|x+n-y|}{s^{3}}e^{-\frac{|x+n-y|^{2}}{2s}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|n_{1}|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{s_{0}}\frac{|x+n-y|}{s^{3}}e^{-\frac{|x+n-y|^{2}}{4s}}e^{-\frac{c_{4}|n_{1}|}{4s}}ds

(B.40)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\\
|n_{1}|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\frac{c_{5}e^{-c_{4}|n_{1}|}}{|x+n-y|^{3}}\leq c_{6}.

(B.41)

This shows (
B.36
). Now by Young’s inequality (
B.36
) implies for
|\beta|\leq 2

\|d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha}\left(\partial^{\beta}\int\Gamma(x,y)f(y)\mathrm{d}y\right)\|_{L^{p}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{p}}

Provided
\alpha>1
, which implies that
f\mapsto\int\int\Gamma(x,y)f(y)\mathrm{d}y
is bounded from
L^{p}
to the weighted Sobolev space
W^{2,p}(d^{1+\delta}(x,\partial M))
,
and similarly one may show boundedness

W^{-1,p}~\mapsto~W^{1,p}(d^{1+\delta}(x,\partial M)).

In the same way one may show that it is also bounded
W^{s,p}\mapsto W^{s+1,p}
(without weight) for
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Now by interpolation (see
[
ADD23
]
) we obtain
\Gamma
is bounded

W^{s,p}\mapsto W^{s+\varepsilon}(d^{2\varepsilon,p}(x,\partial M))

which in turn implies boundedness
W^{s,p}\mapsto W^{s+\varepsilon,q}
for
1/q>1/p+2\varepsilon
by Hölder’s inequality. From this our statement follows by Standard Besov embeddings.
∎

The following lemma follows from above commutator proof.

Lemma B.5
.

\|J_{t}f\|_{B^{s+1-\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{p,p}}\leq C\langle t\rangle^{-1/2-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,p}}

(B.42)

Proof.

This follows from (
B.36
) and standard arguments.
∎

B.5
Regularizing properties of
(C_{T}^{M})^{1/2}

As can be seen by using the Dirichlet basis,
C^{M}_{\infty}
is a positive operator and we denote its square root by
(C^{M}_{\infty})^{1/2}
. We may represent this by the formula,

(C_{\infty}^{M})^{1/2}=\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}e^{-t(-\Delta+m^{2})}dt.

(B.43)

The following lemma follows from
[
CKS10
]
. Note that the asymmetric bound is as in their heat kernel bound.

Lemma B.6
.

For every
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)
, there exists
C>0
such that

|(C_{\infty}^{M})^{1/2}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{d(x,\partial M)^{\alpha_{1}}d(y,\partial M)^{\alpha_{2}}}{|x-y|^{2+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}},\qquad\forall x,y\in M.

(B.44)

Appendix C
Estimates on Poisson kernels

C.1
Massive Poisson kernels and harmonic extension

Let
\overline{H}
denote the
m
-Harmonic extension on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
with associated Poisson kernel
\overline{P}_{0}
. For each
\tau\geq 0
, let
\widehat{\overline{P}}_{0}(\tau,\cdot)
denote its Fourier transform. Explicitly, we have

\widehat{\overline{P}}_{0}(\tau,n)=e^{-|\tau|(4\pi^{2}|n|^{2}+m^{2})^{1/2}},\qquad\forall\tau\in\mathbb{R},\,\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}.

(C.1)

Let
\overline{H}^{*}
denote the adjoint of
\overline{H}
. Its action on smooth functions is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{\overline{H}^{*}}g(n)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|\tau|(4\pi^{2}|n|^{2}+m^{2})^{1/2}}\hat{g}(\tau,n)d\tau,\qquad\forall g\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}),\,\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}.

(C.2)

Let
g\in L^{1}_{\tau}B^{s}_{p,q,z}
. We claim that
\overline{H}^{*}g\in B^{s}_{p,p,z}
. To see this, note that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|\widehat{\overline{H}^{*}}g(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{s}_{p,p,z}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\|g(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{s}_{p,p,z}}d\tau.

(C.3)

Hence
\overline{H}^{*}:L^{1}_{\tau}B^{s}_{p,q,z}\rightarrow B^{s}_{p,q,z}
.

We now have the following lemma

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
\beta\geq 0
, then

\|\overline{H}\varphi(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{\alpha+\beta}_{p,q,z}}\leq C(\beta)\tau^{-\beta}\|\varphi\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q,z}}.

(C.4)

And similarly

\|H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{B^{\alpha+\beta}_{p,q,z}}\leq C(\beta,L)d(\tau,\cdot)^{-\beta}\left(\|\varphi_{-}\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q,z}}+\|\varphi_{+}\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q,z}}\right).

(C.5)

Proof.

To show the first statement observe that by (
C.1
) we have that
\overline{H}(\varphi)(\tau,\cdot)=M_{\tau}\varphi
, where
M_{\tau}
is an
S^{\beta}
multiplier in the sense of
[
BCD11
]
, with multiplier norm being bounded by
\tau^{-\beta}
. For the second statement we observe that (for a cylinder
[0,L]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
)

H(\varphi_{-},\varphi_{+})(\tau,\cdot)=H\left(\overline{H}(\varphi_{-})(L,\cdot),\overline{H}(\varphi_{+})(-L)\right)(\tau,\cdot)+\overline{H}(\varphi_{-})(\tau,\cdot)+\overline{H}(\varphi_{+})(\tau-L,\cdot).

(C.6)

The first term satisfies

\displaystyle\|H\left(\overline{H}(\varphi_{-})(L,\cdot),\overline{H}(\varphi_{+})(-L)\right)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}B^{\alpha+\beta}_{p,q,z}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\overline{H}(1,\cdot)(\varphi_{-})\|_{B^{\alpha+\beta}_{p,q,z}}+\|\overline{H}(0,\cdot)(\varphi_{+})\|_{B^{\alpha+\beta}_{p,q,z}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(L,\beta)\left(\|\varphi_{-}\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q,z}}+\|\varphi_{+}\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q,z}}\right)

where in the last line we have applied (
C.4
). Now we can conclude by triangle inequality.

∎

C.2
Gradient estimate

Lemma C.2
.

For every
\delta>0
, there exists
C>0
such that

|\nabla_{y}C^{B}_{\infty}(x,y)|\leq C\frac{1}{d(y,\partial M)^{2+\delta}}\frac{1}{d(x,\partial M)^{\delta}},\qquad\forall x,y\in M.

(C.7)

Proof.

We drop
\infty
from the notation. By definition we have

\nabla_{y}C^{B}(x,y)=\int_{\partial M}\int_{\partial M}C^{M}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})P(x,\tilde{x})\nabla_{y}P(y,\tilde{y}){\mathrm{d}}\tilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{y}

Now from
[
Kra05
]
we have

\displaystyle|\nabla_{y}C^{B}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial M}\int_{\partial M}|C^{M}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})P(x,\tilde{x})\nabla_{y}P(y,\tilde{y})|{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\partial M}\int_{\partial M}\frac{1}{|\tilde{x}-\tilde{y}|}\frac{d(x,{\partial M})}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{3}}\frac{d(y,{\partial M})}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{4}}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\int_{\partial M}\int_{\partial M}\frac{1}{|\tilde{x}-\tilde{y}|}\frac{1}{|x-\tilde{x}|^{2}}\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|^{3}}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime}\frac{1}{d{(y,{\partial M})^{2+\varepsilon}}}\frac{1}{d(x,{\partial M})^{\varepsilon}}\int_{\partial M}\int_{\partial M}\frac{1}{|\tilde{x}-\tilde{y}|}\frac{1}{|x-\tilde{x}|}\frac{1}{|y-\tilde{y}|}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{x}{\mathrm{d}}\tilde{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime\prime}\frac{1}{d{(y,{\partial M})^{2+\varepsilon}}}\frac{1}{d(x,{\partial M})^{\varepsilon}}.

∎
